I just pick at crackers or have a bowl of cereal, mostly because the idea of cooking for myself just feels pointless right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Um, not really sad, no. I haven't really noticed feeling down in that way. I don't find myself crying or anything like that. It's more just... well, like I said with the cooking, things just feel a bit pointless right now. But I wouldn't say I'm walking around feeling sad most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying activities that you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I still go through the motions with things I used to love... like watering the garden, or showing up to my book club. But most of the time, it just feels flat. I don't really get anything out of it anymore, and I usually end up leaving early, or sometimes I just don't even bother starting at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yes, like I mentioned a minute ago, I have been losing weight without really trying to. Since my appetite has been so unpredictable these past few months, and I'm either skipping dinner entirely or just eating some crackers instead of actually cooking, the weight has just kind of dropped off.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from things that normally would bring you pleasure?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that is exactly what I meant a minute ago. I just don't get any joy out of things right now. Like I said, I'll go out to water the garden or show up to my book club, but the actual feeling of pleasure just isn't there anymore. It all just feels completely flat. I'm really just going through the motions, which is why I end up leaving early or just not bothering to start at all.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with things seeming pointless and not enjoying activities?\n\nPatient: It started right around the time my divorce was finalized. So, that would be about a year and seven months ago now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any feelings of worthlessness?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. I just... I keep replaying the marriage in my head, and I keep telling myself that I must have failed at something really basic. I mean, part of me knows that relationships are much more complicated than that, but I still end up feeling like I just failed.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your lack of interest in activities and feelings of worthlessness are interfering with your daily life most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yes, it definitely is. I mean, I'm still making it to work every day, but... I'm a social worker, and my documentation is just piling up. I keep having to stay late just to fix mistakes or catch up on things that I used to handle so easily. And then outside of work, it's worse. I've just let my friendships and even my basic daily routines shrink down to almost nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel sad or empty inside, even if it's not all the time?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I feel sad, exactly. It's more of just... an empty feeling. Like I'm just going through the motions most days. I don't really cry or feel overwhelmingly sad, I just feel kind of blank.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel sad?\n\nPatient: Like I said, I wouldn't really call it sadness. It's not like I'm sitting around crying or feeling miserable in that way. It's just... it's just this emptiness. I don't really feel much of anything right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad?\n\nPatient: I think you just asked me that... but no, I really don't feel sad. Like I was just saying, it's not sadness. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, what finally made me come in is that my appetite has just been all over the place for months now. There are stretches where I just skip dinner entirely and end up losing weight without even trying to. And then other nights... I just pick at crackers or have a bowl of cereal, mostly because the idea of cooking for myself just feels pointless right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Um, not really sad, no. I haven't really noticed feeling down in that way. I don't find myself crying or anything like that. It's more just... well, like I said with the cooking, things just feel a bit pointless right now. But I wouldn't say I'm walking around feeling sad most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying activities that you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I still go through the motions with things I used to love... like watering the garden, or showing up to my book club. But most of the time, it just feels flat. I don't really get anything out of it anymore, and I usually end up leaving early, or sometimes I just don't even bother starting at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yes, like I mentioned a minute ago, I have been losing weight without really trying to. Since my appetite has been so unpredictable these past few months, and I'm either skipping dinner entirely or just eating some crackers instead of actually cooking, the weight has just kind of dropped off.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from things that normally would bring you pleasure?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that is exactly what I meant a minute ago. I just don't get any joy out of things right now. Like I said, I'll go out to water the garden or show up to my book club, but the actual feeling of pleasure just isn't there anymore. It all just feels completely flat. I'm really just going through the motions, which is why I end up leaving early or just not bothering to start at all.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with things seeming pointless and not enjoying activities?\n\nPatient: It started right around the time my divorce was finalized. So, that would be about a year and seven months ago now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any feelings of worthlessness?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. I just... I mean, I'm still making it to work every day, but... I'm a social worker, and my documentation is just piling up. It's not like I'm sitting around crying or feeling miserable in that way. It's just... it's just this emptiness. I don't really feel much of anything right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad?\n\nPatient: I think you just asked me that... but no, I really don't feel sad. Like I was just saying, it's not sadness. It's just a completely empty feeling. I'm not really sure how else to explain it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the day?\n\nPatient: I feel like we're going in circles here. No, I don't feel sad most of the day. Like I've been saying, I don't feel sad at all. It's just that constant empty feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would say you're feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if I'd use that word. I guess I just don't know. I'm really just going through the motions, which is why I end up leaving early or just not bothering to start at all.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with things seeming pointless and not enjoying activities?\n\nPatient: It started right around the time my divorce was finalized. So, that would be about a year and seven months ago now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any feelings of worthlessness?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. I just... I keep replaying the marriage in my head, and I keep telling myself that I must have failed at something really basic. I mean, part of me knows that relationships are much more complicated than that, but I still end up feeling like I just failed.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your lack of interest in activities and feelings of worthlessness are interfering with your daily life most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yes, it definitely is. I mean, I'm still making it to work every day, but... I'm a social worker, and my documentation is just piling up. I keep having to stay late just to fix mistakes or catch up on things that I used to handle so easily. And then outside of work, it's worse. I've just let my friendships and even my basic daily routines shrink down to almost nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel sad or empty inside, even if it's not all the time?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I feel sad, exactly. It's more of just... an empty feeling. Like I'm just going through the motions most days. I don't really cry or feel overwhelmingly sad, I just feel kind of blank.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel sad?\n\nPatient: Like I said, I wouldn't really call it sadness. It's not like I'm sitting around crying or feeling miserable in that way. It's just... it's just this emptiness. I don't really feel much of anything right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad?\n\nPatient: I think you just asked me that... but no, I really don't feel sad. Like I was just saying, it's not sadness. It's just a completely empty feeling. I'm not really sure how else to explain it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the day?\n\nPatient: I feel like we're going in circles here. No, I don't feel sad most of the day. Like I've been saying, I don't feel sad at all. It's just that constant empty feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would say you're feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if I'd use that word. I guess I just don't know. Like I was saying a minute ago, it's really just this flat, empty feeling. That's what I experience most days. Just flat.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel depressed?\n\nPatient: I... I really don't know how else to answer that. We just went over this. No, I don't feel depressed. I'm really just going through the motions, which is why I end up leaving early or just not bothering to start at all.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with things seeming pointless and not enjoying activities?\n\nPatient: It started right around the time my divorce was finalized. I'm really just going through the motions, which is why I end up leaving early or just not bothering to start at all.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with things seeming pointless and not enjoying activities?\n\nPatient: It started right around the time my divorce was finalized. So, that would be about a year and seven months ago now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any feelings of worthlessness?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. I just... I keep replaying the marriage in my head, and I keep telling myself that I must have failed at something really basic. I mean, part of me knows that relationships are much more complicated than that, but I still end up feeling like I just failed.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your lack of interest in activities and feelings of worthlessness are interfering with your daily life most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yes, it definitely is. I mean, I'm still making it to work every day, but... I'm a social worker, and my documentation is just piling up. I keep having to stay late just to fix mistakes or catch up on things that I used to handle so easily. And then outside of work, it's worse. I've just let my friendships and even my basic daily routines shrink down to almost nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel sad or empty inside, even if it's not all the time?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I feel sad, exactly. It's more of just... an empty feeling. Like I'm just going through the motions most days. I don't really cry or feel overwhelmingly sad, I just feel kind of blank.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel sad?\n\nPatient: Like I said, I wouldn't really call it sadness. It's not like I'm sitting around crying or feeling miserable in that way. It's just... it's just this emptiness. I don't really feel much of anything right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad?\n\nPatient: I think you just asked me that... but no, I really don't feel sad. Like I was just saying, it's not sadness. It's just a completely empty feeling. I'm not really sure how else to explain it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the day?\n\nPatient: I feel like we're going in circles here. No, I don't feel sad most of the day. Like I've been saying, I don't feel sad at all. It's just that constant empty feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would say you're feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if I'd use that word. I guess I just don't know. Like I was saying a minute ago, it's really just this flat, empty feeling. I don't know if that means I'm depressed, but... that's just what I'm experiencing right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're depressed most days?\n\nPatient: I mean... we really are just repeating the exact same thing now. No, I wouldn't say I feel depressed most days, because like I just told you, I don't really know if I'd even use the word depressed to begin with. It's just... it's just that emptiness. That's what I experience most days. Just flat.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel depressed?\n\nPatient: I... I really don't know how else to answer that. We just went over this. No, I don't feel depressed. I don't know what else to tell you besides what I just said about having that same flat, empty feeling. My answer hasn't changed.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel depressed?\n\nPatient: I... I really don't know what you want me to say at this point. You are asking me the exact same question again. Like I've been telling you, no, I don't feel depressed. I just feel empty. I don't have a different answer to give you.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your empty feeling and lack of interest in activities are present most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: The empty feeling, yes. That is there most of the day, pretty much every day. But... as for a lack of interest in activities, I haven't really noticed that. I wouldn't say I've lost interest in doing things, it's really just that flat feeling I mentioned that stays with me.\n\nDoctor: For how long have you been feeling like you're just going through the motions with activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I think maybe you misunderstood what I just said. I haven't really lost interest in the things I enjoy, so I don't feel like I'm just going through the motions with them. Like I mentioned a moment ago, it's really just that empty feeling I have.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel depressed most of the day?\n\nPatient: I feel like we are going in circles here.
